Kennedy, joined by Thomas, concurs while emphasizing that Biggins brought disparate treatment alone. He regards importing Title VII disparate-impact doctrine into the ADEA as presenting substantial unresolved objections and understands the Court to decide none of them. The Court’s express reservation supports his join; his skepticism is not a holding. Stone does not join this additional theory.

#### Newark Morning Ledger Co. v. United States, 1993-04-20

Source: [record’s published writings](records/newark_merits_1993-04-20.md).

| Writing | Author | Parts and exact joins | Judgment relationship |
|---|---|---|---|
| Opinion of the Court | Blackmun | I: governing rule; II: proved asset and remand. Joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Thomas. | Reversal |
| Dissent | Souter | Entire. Joined by White, Scalia. | Would affirm |

Souter, joined by White and Scalia, would affirm on two grounds. First, the regulation categorically denies depreciation of goodwill, and at-will subscribers’ expected continued purchases are that goodwill; redefining it as whatever remains after measurable assets are removed changes the accepted rule. Second, even under the Court’s test, measuring how long existing subscribers continue does not establish how long the purchased pre-sale goodwill lasts rather than being sustained by post-sale editorial and business efforts. The conditional stipulation concedes neither issue. These independent objections are noncontrolling; the majority accepts the trial findings under the proof-based construction.

#### Nebraska v. Wyoming, 1993-04-20

Source: [record’s published writings](records/nebraska_partial_summary_judgment_1993-04-20.md).

| Writing | Author | Parts and exact joins | Judgment relationship |
|---|---|---|---|
| Opinion of the Court | O'Connor | I: enforcement/modification and Rule 56; II-A: decree-based Inland Lakes right; III–V: remaining components and remedy. Joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas. | Unanimous stated component dispositions |
| Opinion of the Court | O'Connor | II-B: independently sufficient postdecree-acquiescence ground. Joined by White, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas. | Eight-Justice alternative ground; Stone does not join |

No separate writing. The Chief’s limited join leaves the independent acquiescence ground supported by eight Justices. Stone joins the intervention denial in the judgment only and adds no ground.

#### Brecht v. Abrahamson, 1993-04-21

Source: [record’s published writings](records/brecht_merits_1993-04-21.md).

| Writing | Author | Parts and exact joins | Judgment relationship |
|---|---|---|---|
| Opinion of the Court | White | I: constitutional trial error. Joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas. | Unanimous classification |
| Opinion of the Court | White | II–III: Chapman for this claim; vacatur and conditional-remedy directions. Joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, O'Connor, Souter. | Five-Justice controlling rule |
| Concurrence | White | A: broader continuity argument. Joined by Blackmun, Souter. | Souter joins without taking a position on Powell’s merits |
| Concurrence | White | B: objection to rigid structural/trial taxonomy. Joined by Blackmun. | Noncontrolling methodological reservation |
| Concurrence | O'Connor | Entire. No other join. | Reliability and administration grounds; joins the Court |
| Dissent | Kennedy | Entire. Joined by Scalia, Thomas. | Would affirm using Kotteakos |
| Dissent | Stevens | Entire. No other join. | Would affirm using demanding whole-record Kotteakos review |

White, joined by Blackmun and by Souter in Part A with an express reservation of Powell’s merits, concurs that section 2254 and the Court’s constitutional practice more broadly resist giving the same error different remedial force solely because direct review has ended. White and Blackmun alone also question the reliability of a rigid trial-error/structural-defect taxonomy; that reservation does not undo their join in the classification of this Doyle error. O’Connor concurs on the importance of confidence in verdict accuracy and the administrative costs of multiplying harmless-error categories. She accepts that direct and collateral review can differ, but finds no sufficient justification for diluting Chapman here.

Kennedy, joined by Scalia and Thomas, would affirm. Finality, comity and the limited collateral role justify asking whether the violation substantially and injuriously affected the verdict under Kotteakos. Lawful prewarning impeachment and the physical and circumstantial evidence make the improper references cumulative on this record. The dissent does not recast Doyle as nonconstitutional or treat structural defects as ordinary trial errors. It leaves open, without adopting an exception, whether an especially egregious deliberate error or a pattern of misconduct could independently undermine the proceeding’s integrity despite no substantial verdict effect. Stevens separately would affirm under Kotteakos, as anticipated by his Greer concurrence. He emphasizes a demanding independent review of the entire record, the prosecution’s obligation to explain harmlessness and the error’s actual influence on the factfinder rather than a bare sufficiency-of-evidence test. His reasoning and the principal dissent remain noncontrolling.

#### Withrow v. Williams, 1993-04-21

Source: [record’s published writings](records/withrow_merits_1993-04-21.md).

| Writing | Author | Parts and exact joins | Judgment relationship |
|---|---|---|---|
| Opinion of the Court | Souter | I–II: Miranda review and corresponding remand. Joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, Kennedy. | Six-Justice holding |
| Opinion of the Court | Souter | III: notice and opportunity on the independent theory. Joined by Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas. | Unanimous holding |
| Concurrence in part and dissent | O'Connor | Entire. No other join. | Joins III; would extend Powell |
| Concurrence in part and dissent | Scalia | Entire. Joined by Thomas. | Joins III; would assess exceptional equitable grounds before relitigation |

O’Connor concurs in the notice reversal and dissents on Miranda review. Following her Duckworth concurrence, she would extend Powell to a claim involving voluntary but unwarned statements after a full and fair state opportunity. She regards involuntariness review as the appropriate means to remedy genuine coercion and the added deterrent value of later Miranda review as insufficient to outweigh finality and retrial costs. This does not reject collateral review of actual compelled statements.

Scalia, joined by Thomas, also joins the notice ruling but would remand for a determination whether unusual equitable circumstances justify renewed Miranda litigation after the opportunities already afforded. He treats prior full and fair litigation as a powerful equitable consideration under sections 2241 and 2243, subject to considerations concerning fairness or accuracy, rather than a jurisdictional defect. The separate writing urges a broader approach beyond this case; the Court does not adopt it. It does not cite an unentered historical Brecht rule or convert the simulated Herrera’s limited record holding into a categorical innocence bar.

#### CSX Transportation, Inc. v. Easterwood, 1993-04-21

Source: [record’s published writings](records/easterwood_merits_1993-04-21.md).

| Writing | Author | Parts and exact joins | Judgment relationship |
|---|---|---|---|
| Opinion of the Court | White | I: section 434 coverage; II: warning devices. Joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas. | Unanimous warning disposition and governing coverage rule |
| Opinion of the Court | White | III–IV: ordinary speed application and disposition. Joined by Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy. | Six-Justice speed holding |
| Concurrence in part and dissent | Thomas | Entire. Joined by Souter. | Joins I–II; would allow speed claim |
| Concurrence in part and dissent | Stone-Zsela | Entire. No other join. | Joins I–II; would vacate speed ruling for the bounded inquiry |

Thomas, joined by Souter, concurs on warning devices and dissents on speed. He reads the track-class maximum as addressing track safety rather than substantially subsuming every crossing-specific speed duty. The contemporaneous rulemaking’s exclusion of population and crossing variables, and the reservation of other slowing duties, defeat broader coverage in his view. He would let the pleaded speed claim proceed; he does not announce liability.

Stone-Zsela concurs on warning devices and the statutory coverage method but would vacate the categorical speed ruling for a limited inquiry into the duty actually pleaded and preserved. A demand merely to substitute a lower ordinary operating-speed limit remains preempted. A distinct preserved crossing-condition duty may proceed only if its subject is not substantially covered by operative regulations; general time-and-place allegations do not establish that distinction. The remand may not manufacture a new theory or treat separately pleaded vegetation or hump issues as an unestablished speed claim. If no qualifying theory exists, speed preemption remains. He leaves the specific-individual-hazard question reserved and requires independent satisfaction of every local-hazard condition for any reliance on that saving clause. His remand position is neither the six-Justice affirmance nor the two-Justice position allowing the speed claim outright, and it is noncontrolling.

#### United States v. Olano — 1993-04-26

Kennedy joins the Court. He emphasizes that the defendant’s burden is the usual framework, not necessarily the formulation for an error noticed by the court on its own initiative. Rule 24(c) remains independently obligatory; trial judges should not put counsel in the position of resisting a proposed departure from the prescribed jury structure. A preserved objection would place the burden of showing harmlessness on the Government, which could face substantial difficulty. None of those observations permits withholding an objection and demanding automatic reversal later.

Stevens, joined by White and Blackmun, would affirm. Drawing on Vasquez v. Hillery and Gray v. Mississippi, he regards the privacy of deliberations as protecting the tribunal’s integrity independently of measurable verdict prejudice. He nonetheless distinguishes satisfaction of substantial rights from discretionary relief. A reasonable judge could decline reversal after considering the nature of the violation, counsel’s conduct and likely effect; his conclusion is that the Ninth Circuit did not abuse its broad supervisory discretion by granting relief here.

Source: published writings in [Decision Record](records/olano_merits_1993-04-26.md); all separate propositions are noncontrolling except for the Court joins expressly identified in Current Law.

#### Edenfield v. Fane — 1993-04-26

Blackmun joins the opinion and judgment but maintains that truthful, nonmisleading commercial speech deserves more protection than the intermediate framework affords; that broader view is not adopted. O’Connor dissents. Her Shapero and Peel positions support attention to the culture and responsibilities of a profession, not simply the sophistication of a particular prospective client. She would allow Florida to protect independence and prevent pressure through a prophylactic restriction without waiting for proven abuse. In her view the Court understates experience-based risks and permits an injunction extending to business solicitation generally on a record centered on Fane’s proposed conduct. She would reverse; no Justice joins her writing.

Source: published writings in [Decision Record](records/edenfield_merits_1993-04-26.md); all separate propositions are noncontrolling except for the Court joins expressly identified in Current Law.

#### United States v. Idaho ex rel. Director, Idaho Department of Water Resources — 1993-05-03

Stone, joined by Stevens, agrees that the assessments cannot be imposed. Idaho finances the adjudication by substituting the challenged assessment for conventional costs; Congress expressly excluded judgments for costs. That textual ground suffices. The concurrence would not turn the result into a general rule that every procedural fee requires a separately phrased waiver. Nor would it permit the Government to avoid ordinary state procedures or the comprehensive adjudication itself. The concurrence supports the cost-characterization ground and judgment, but does not join the Court’s broader monetary-waiver reasoning. Stevens’ position is consistent with his Nordic Village dissent because the present statute contains an express exclusion.

Source: published writings in [Decision Record](records/idaho_merits_1993-05-03.md); all separate propositions are noncontrolling except for the Court joins expressly identified in Current Law.

#### Stinson v. United States — 1993-05-03

Stone concurs in the judgment, independently reading the applicable guideline to exclude felon possession alone. He preserves Williams’ rule for departure policy statements and the statutory role of commentary in assessing considered departure factors. Those rules do not establish mandatory deference to every later interpretation of a classification provision. Guidelines undergo congressional review under §994(p), while commentary changes do not; judicial interpretation must remain independently justified. Under a Skidmore-type approach the Commission’s expertise, consistency, text and reasoning receive substantial weight. Stone does not limit crime of violence to offenses with force elements: the serious-risk clause must be read with burglary of a dwelling, arson, extortion and use of explosives. Possession alone does not establish comparable inherent risk merely because a prohibited possessor might later misuse the firearm. That legal error, rather than a request simply to reconsider commentary already considered, supports vacatur. An explanation can illuminate what original text meant without retroactively creating a rule; a substantive later change must satisfy its own temporal conditions. The lower court must identify the governing version, address the preserved temporal argument and any properly supported alternative ground, and apply Williams to the error’s consequences. No particular sentence or final-case reopening follows. No associate joins this writing; its persuasive-weight framework is noncontrolling.

Source: published writings in [Decision Record](records/stinson_merits_1993-05-03.md); all separate propositions are noncontrolling except for the Court joins expressly identified in Current Law.

#### Professional Real Estate Investors, Inc. v. Columbia Pictures Industries, Inc. — 1993-05-03

Souter joins the Court. Objective probable cause provides a useful threshold, but the decision does not incorporate every detail of malicious-prosecution law or Rule11 practice into antitrust immunity. Stevens, joined by O’Connor, agrees that this tenable copyright action cannot sustain the counterclaim. He regards the broader formulation as unnecessary to that result and warns that objectively plausible proceedings can sometimes be used as part of abusive conduct. A pattern, imposed burdens or conduct distinct from a genuine attempt to obtain relief may require a different inquiry. The concurrence would decide this record without foreclosing those possibilities. Neither separate writing changes the seven-Justice rule governing the single suit and theory actually reviewed.

Source: published writings in [Decision Record](records/pre_merits_1993-05-03.md); all separate propositions are noncontrolling except for the Court joins expressly identified in Current Law.

#### Cardinal Chemical Co. v. Morton International, Inc. — 1993-05-17

Scalia, joined by Souter, joins PartsI–III and the judgment. He agrees that the independent validity counterclaim is not automatically moot and that the jurisdictional premise of the Federal Circuit’s practice fails. He does not join PartIV. Because both parties oppose the automatic practice, the scope of appellate discretion has not received the same adversary development. He would remand for the Federal Circuit to exercise genuine discretion in the first instance, while rejecting a conclusion that jurisdiction itself forces every validity question to be decided. The seven-Justice PartIV nevertheless governs this record, with its express preservation of unusual case-specific grounds.

Source: published writings in [Decision Record](records/cardinal_merits_1993-05-17.md); all separate propositions are noncontrolling except for the Court joins expressly identified in Current Law.

#### Deal v. United States — 1993-05-17

**Dissent — Stevens; joined by Blackmun, O'Connor:** Stevens, joined by Blackmun and O’Connor, reads the phrase as a recidivist provision addressing renewed offending after a prior conviction. The long-established operation of repeat-offender statutes and the development of §924(c) matter more than abstractly counting findings of guilt. Bell, Simpson and Busic support resolving the remaining uncertainty against multiplying the aggravated tier in the first prosecution. The dissent objects that the Court’s construction makes the enhancement depend on the prosecutor’s decision to collect counts in one case and produces repeated enhanced punishment without an intervening opportunity to conform conduct after conviction. It would preserve the underlying convictions and consecutive base penalties. This view does not control. Stevens’s discussion invokes an earlier conviction becoming final; Stone’s distinct dissent expressly leaves appellate finality undecided and is not a join in that language.

**Dissent — Stone-Zsela; no other joins:** Stone-Zsela would require a qualifying conviction before commission of the later offense. He acknowledges that the Government’s ordinary ordinal reading is substantial, but reads the phrase in its recidivist setting and statutory development. Bell, Simpson and Busic support disciplined construction without themselves deciding the present phrase. Residual uncertainty is resolved through lenity after considering those tools. The proposed remedy removes only the aggravated tiers, retaining all six convictions, the consecutive-service command and unrelated sentencing components. He does not decide that the prior conviction must be final on appeal and does not adopt a proportionality limit. His separate writing is noncontrolling.

#### Commissioner v. Keystone Consolidated Industries, Inc. — 1993-05-24

**Dissent — Stevens; no other joins:** Stevens would affirm because income-tax treatment does not necessarily settle the different prohibited-transaction provision. Both voluntary property contributions and transfers satisfying minimum funding can produce income-tax gain; borrowing that classification selectively gives insufficient weight to ERISA’s context. The statutory emphasis on encumbered transfers and the distinction between genuine plan funding and transactions extracting plan assets support the narrower reading. Trustees remain responsible for deciding whether to accept suitable property, which can benefit a plan rather than threaten it. Atlantic Cleaners cautions that identical words may bear different meanings in different settings. This disagreement concerns statutory classification, not a finding that Keystone’s properties were misvalued, a general exemption for every employer transaction, or an order computing tax. The dissent does not control.

Scalia and Stone withhold the Court’s supporting policy portion while joining the ordinary-exchange rule; no separate policy rule or additional opinion is implied.

#### Keene Corp. v. United States — 1993-05-24

**Dissent — Stevens; no other joins:** Stevens reads the pendency language in light of the statute’s purpose of preventing simultaneous pursuit of duplicative claims. Once the other action has ended, that purpose no longer requires closing the remaining forum. He objects that a rigid filing-time rule can defeat adjudication even though neither court is then considering a competing suit and each forum initially had different jurisdictional limits. The lower decisions permitting a plaintiff to remove the obstacle before adjudication reinforce his treatment of the statute as an election rather than an irrevocable trap. He would not approve duplicate recovery or concurrent adjudication; his disagreement concerns the ability to proceed after the competing action ends. The dissent supplies no current exception to the Court’s filing-date rule.

#### Smith v. United States — 1993-06-01

**Concurrence — Blackmun; no other joins:** Blackmun joins the Court’s construction and its application to the offered exchange. He emphasizes that this firearm was tendered as the consideration for the cocaine, so its relationship to the charged trafficking was established without deciding every marginal form of facilitation. He does not understand the opinion’s minimum formulation to make facilitation alone sufficient in every case. That reservation preserves the separate relation-to element and leaves its outer boundary open; it does not withhold a vote from the result or from the Court’s rule for this transaction.

**Dissent — Scalia; joined by Stevens, Souter:** Scalia, joined by Stevens and Souter, reads the entire phrase in its ordinary setting: using a firearm means employing it as a weapon, not merely doing something useful with it. The argument does not restrict weapon use to firing; threats or striking can be weapon employment. The broader transaction language in the forfeiture provision addresses different objects and does not establish the criminal element in §924(c). Treating the gun as merchandise lacks the contextual weapon use charged here, even if trading it assists the drug transaction. The separate relation-to requirement cannot cure that missing element. The dissent would reverse the firearm-use conviction and cannot sustain it on carrying, which was not charged. It does not control.

**Dissent — Stone-Zsela; no other joins:** Stone-Zsela accepts the force of the Government’s instrumental-use reading but finds that the criminal phrase in context addresses employment as a weapon. Perrin and Timbers require contextual ordinary meaning; Bass and Moskal call for lenity only after the ordinary tools leave genuine uncertainty. The independent connection to trafficking does not answer whether the gun was used in the charged sense. Threats, intimidation or striking may qualify without firing, whereas merchandise-only barter does not. He would remove the firearm count and its thirty-year mandatory term and remand for the resulting sentence correction. The separate drug convictions remain, and the remand would not authorize conviction for uncharged carrying or a new trial to cure the rejected legal theory. This position is noncontrolling.

#### Mertens v. Hewitt Associates — 1993-06-01

**Dissent — White; joined by Stevens, O'Connor:** White, joined by Stevens and O’Connor, reads equitable relief in the particular trust-law setting that ERISA incorporates. Firestone makes that background central, and Clews illustrates the traditional power of equity to restore trust losses through a monetary award, including against a knowing participant. The dissent regards the majority’s general legal-versus-equitable categories as an insufficient account of this particular remedy. It argues that the equitable limitation still matters because punitive relief and remedies arising only from the later merger of law and equity were not traditional trust restoration. Russell does not decide the different §502(a)(3) issue. The express redress language and §502(l)’s treatment of knowing participants further support a claim against a participating nonfiduciary without making every professional a fiduciary. This position would remove the categorical dismissal, not establish liability or an amount of recovery. It does not control.

**Dissent — Stone-Zsela; no other joins:** Stone-Zsela would permit traditional monetary restoration for losses caused by actual knowing participation in an ERISA fiduciary’s breach. Firestone supports consulting trust law, and Clews identifies this restorative remedy as equitable; the argument is not that every award once obtainable incidentally in equity becomes available. Russell’s respect for the statutory enforcement structure remains, but does not settle the distinct redress provision presented here. Section502(a)(3)’s express authority to redress the fiduciary violation supplies the bounded cause; §502(l) offers context without creating an independent private action. Hewitt’s disclaimer is a presentation limit, not a jurisdictional flaw. On remand the participants would still need to prove breach, actual knowing participation and causal loss, and the court would determine the proper amount and recipient within equitable limits. No professional-negligence or punitive claim is authorized. Fixed nonfiduciary status, unchallenged restitution, unappealed parties and claims, and the separate malpractice disposition remain closed. His conclusion on statutory coverage is a dissenting proposition, not an answer supplied by the Court.

#### Musick, Peeler & Garrett v. Employers Insurance of Wausau — 1993-06-01

**Dissent — Thomas; joined by Blackmun, O'Connor:** Thomas, joined by Blackmun and O’Connor, distinguishes the accepted private action of an injured investor from a new action by a wrongdoer seeking reimbursement. Northwest Airlines and Texas Industries require congressional authorization rather than a judicial preference for fair allocation. The existence of contribution in other express securities sections shows that Congress knew how to provide it; it does not itself enact a contribution right under §10(b). Lampf’s task of defining a limitation period for a received action does not necessarily justify creating a distinct claimant and claim. The dissent would reject the asserted federal contribution right without deciding whether these professionals committed securities violations or fixing anyone’s share of loss. Its disagreement concerns remedial authority, not a general rule immunizing advisers from their own otherwise established liability. It is noncontrolling.

### 5. Current Procedure and Institution

Nine Justices seated throughout these completed events: Chief Stone-Zsela; associates White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas in seniority order. Quorum six; ordinary full-Court action requires five of nine; certiorari requires four affirmative votes. Event-specific nonparticipation is recorded in the relevant decision and does not alter the seated roster. Allotments effective October 23, 1991 remain: Stone D.C./Fourth/Federal; Souter First; Scalia Second/Third; O'Connor Fifth; Stevens Sixth/Seventh; Blackmun Eighth; Kennedy Ninth; White Tenth; Thomas Eleventh. Listed interim applications refer to the full Court; ordinary petitions and IFP filing motions are not routed through a fictitious Circuit Justice referral.

Grubbs's administrative stay remains operative pending further Court order. Its application requires prompt examination of the actual application and State response; no long-term stay, merits disposition or automatic October 21 dissolution is inferred. Coleman v. Thompson is not an OT1992 matter; it is resolved within OT1991 by post-close correction. Zatko, Wyoming and Reynolds retain the opening states above. Martin's lead petition is closed; its companion fee motion is complete, with the underlying petition still open pending a verified compliance or subsequent Court event. Each completed merits remand retains the case-specific remedy and unresolved issues in its record; remand does not imply a new Supreme Court filing or continuing retained jurisdiction.

- **Grubbs v. Delo:** Administrative stay operative; actual application and State response must be examined promptly; continued relief requires lawful review authority and the ordinary stay showing.
- **Martin v. District of Columbia Court of Appeals and Martin v. McDermott:** The November 2 order grants IFP and denies certiorari in No. 92-5584. That petition proceeding is closed; the D.C. Circuit judgment remains undisturbed. In No. 92-5618, only the fee motion is decided. Martin may pay the Rule 38 docketing fee and submit a petition complying with Rule 33 by November 23, 1992. Ordinary petition processing requires that compliance; the order neither denies the petition nor commands an automatic later denial. No payment, default, extension or subsequent Court action is established. The Delaware judgment is not vacated, affirmed on the merits or remanded. No prospective filing restriction issues. At the continuing term cursor, November 23 has passed, but calendar passage alone supplies no terminal event; No. 92-5618 remains an open compliance-dependent matter.
- **Montana v. Imlay:** Supreme Court writ terminated; later sentence untouched.
- **Church of Scientology of California v. United States:** Supreme Court review complete; Ninth Circuit summons-enforcement appeal restored on remand.
- **Hadley v. United States:** Supreme Court review concluded by DIG; Ninth Circuit judgment remains undisturbed.
- **Parke v. Raley:** Sixth Circuit conditional writ reversed; district rejection of present habeas challenge stands; no new hearing required.
- **Richmond v. Lewis:** Federal district conditional habeas order on remand; Arizona must cure sentence within reasonable period or vacate death/impose lawful lesser sentence.
- **Soldal v. Cook County:** Supreme Court merits review complete; seizure claim remanded for further proceedings below. No retained Supreme Court matter or later filing is established.
- **Farrar v. Hobby:** Supreme Court review complete; fee judgment vacated and amount review remanded. No retained Supreme Court proceeding or later filing established.
- **District of Columbia v. Greater Washington Board of Trade:** Supreme Court review complete; affirmed appellate judgment governs further district-court proceedings in the preemption action.
- **Mississippi v. Louisiana:** Supreme Court merits review complete; interstate complaint must be dismissed below; remaining title proceedings remanded. No new original action or retained Supreme Court matter.
- **Republic National Bank of Miami v. United States:** Supreme Court merits review of the jurisdictional dismissal complete; forfeiture merits appeal remanded to the Eleventh Circuit. No retained Supreme Court matter or actual payment order.

- **Puerto Rico Aqueduct & Sewer Authority v. Metcalf & Eddy, Inc., 1993-01-12:** Supreme Court review of appellate jurisdiction is complete. The Court does not direct that PRASA be declared immune, dismiss the contract action, award damages, or order any particular result on the contract merits. No retained Supreme Court proceeding or new filing deadline is created.
- **Bath Iron Works Corp. v. Director, Office of Workers’ Compensation Programs, 1993-01-12:** Supreme Court merits review is complete. The First Circuit judgment is affirmed; Brown retains the award. The calculation using the 1985 national wage remains uncorrected because the objection was waived, not because it is the lawful general wage rule for scheduled hearing loss. The 82.4 percent figure was not challenged, and the Court awards no new amount or remand recalculation. Retirement audiograms may identify the compensable loss; the Court imposes no mandatory testing duty and decides no new aging-apportionment dispute.
- **Commissioner v. Soliman, 1993-01-12:** Supreme Court merits review is complete. No automatic status, compulsory counsel appointment, merits relief, new damages remedy or predetermined finding of indigence is ordered.

- **Deal v. United States, 1993-05-17:** The reviewed sentence is affirmed: five years on the first firearm conviction and twenty years on each of five additional firearm convictions, all consecutive, for 105 years under §924(c). No conviction, other sentencing component or constitutional proportionality issue is adjudicated. The dissenting proposed base terms would total thirty years under §924(c), consecutively, but that is not the mandate.
- **El Vocero de Puerto Rico v. Puerto Rico, 1993-05-17:** The judgment sustaining blanket closure is reversed and the matter remanded for proceedings consistent with qualified public access. - **Commissioner v. Keystone Consolidated Industries, Inc., 1993-05-24:** The judgment excluding the transfers from §4975(c)(1)(A) is reversed and the case expressly remanded for proceedings under the correct classification and applicable tax conditions. Unencumbered status alone supplies no exclusion. No new valuation, final tax computation, unpresented exemption or automatic second-tier assessment is adjudicated; statutory correction and exemption conditions continue to govern if properly applicable.
- **United States Department of Justice v. Landano, 1993-05-24:** The reviewed judgment is vacated and confidentiality determinations remanded under the express-assurance or supported-inference standard. The agency retains its burden; the requester may rebut an inference using nonprotected evidence. In-camera affidavits or other protective procedures may be used when justified, not required in every case. Reasonably segregable nonexempt material must be released, but deleting a name does not alone require release of protected information furnished by a confidential criminal-investigation source. No blanket disclosure, retrial or innocence finding is ordered.
- **Lincoln v. Vigil, 1993-05-24:** The judgment is reversed and remanded. The lower court may not compel this allocation on the rejected APA grounds. The distinct due-process claim remains unresolved; the Court does not decide the §552 publication question, the independently unreviewed remedial question, or an entitlement to a particular hearing. The decision supplies no authority to spend outside the enacted mission or disregard an independently enforceable legal restriction.
- **Keene Corp. v. United States, 1993-05-24:** The jurisdictional dismissals of Keene I and Keene II are affirmed. No asbestos liability, indemnification entitlement or compensation amount is decided. Later termination of the other actions does not revive jurisdiction in these original suits. The Court gives no advisory decision on a future action or equitable tolling and orders no transfer or new filing opportunity.
- **Sullivan v. Louisiana, 1993-06-01:** The Louisiana judgment sustaining the conviction is reversed and remanded for proceedings permitting a new trial under a constitutionally valid reasonable-doubt charge. No acquittal or sufficiency ruling is entered. The prior penalty-phase relief is undisturbed; the original death sentence is not reinstated. Any sentence after a new conviction must follow the lawful proceedings then required. No retrial date or remedy for unrelated convictions is set.
- **Smith v. United States, 1993-06-01:** The firearm-use conviction and resulting thirty-year consecutive term are affirmed. The Court does not adjudicate the separate drug convictions or an uncharged carrying theory. No completed exchange is required on the trafficking offenses and offer proved here. The dissent’s proposed removal of the firearm count and sentence is not the mandate.
- **Mertens v. Hewitt Associates, 1993-06-01:** The rejection of the reviewed §502(a)(3) compensatory monetary claim is affirmed. No liability finding or award is entered. Nonfiduciary status, the unchallenged restitution ruling, unappealed claims and parties, and the distinct state malpractice disposition remain undisturbed. The antecedent availability of a §502(a)(3) action against this nonfiduciary is reserved, not rejected as a jurisdictional defect.
- **Musick, Peeler & Garrett v. Employers Insurance of Wausau, 1993-06-01:** The judgment permitting the contribution action to proceed is affirmed. The lower proceedings must establish underlying shared liability and the applicable allocation requirements. No contribution amount, professional liability, indemnity right, automatic aiding-and-abetting rule or conclusive effect for every settlement is adjudicated. The Supreme Court review ends without a special mandate date.

### 6. Blockers and Revalidation Needs

No chunk7 matter is stopped and no chunk7 source or Stone-choice blocker remains. Existing pending stages are not terminally resolved by this statement. Moreau and Demos are each completed by correction; Coleman v. Thompson is removed from this term entirely, resolved within OT1991 by post-close correction (`terms/OT1991/records/Coleman_v_Thompson_petition_disposition_1992-06-15.md`). No completed earlier event is changed.

Moreau source: [Official statutory text and record, 508 U.S.22, 25 n.2 and 33 n.17](https://tile.loc.gov/storage-services/service/ll/usrep/usrep508/usrep508022/usrep508022.pdf); [judgment below, 956 F.2d516](https://static.case.law/f2d/956/html/0516-01.html). The transition is a statutory command; state inability to make the representative agreement is the lower court’s construction of Texas law, including the local-adoption exception, not a federal declaration that all Texas bargaining is forbidden. Both the earlier-hire transition-rule component and the later-hire individual-agreement component are now decided; see the record.

Demos source: [original petition and lower orders](https://archive.org/download/micro_IA40385013_0424/micro_IA40385013_0424%202.%20Petition%20for%20Writ%20of%20Certiorari.pdf); sanitized source validation and frozen handoffs are retained under tmp/ot1992-chunk4. The official-interference allegation is not a finding of fact; it was found sufficiently colorable to defeat a Rule 39.8 frivolousness determination, per the record. The pre-divergence In re Demos extraordinary-writ order remains effective within its actual, narrower scope; unentered historical post-divergence fee denials are not simulated Court actions.

Grubbs, the Martin companion, Zatko, Wyoming, Reynolds and Louisiana retain their already recorded open stages or implementation jurisdiction. Further action requires its own authorized materials or verified filing; passage of a compliance date does not create a disposition. McDermott has now been completed against its required March 23 baseline. Delaware v. New York and Nebraska v. Wyoming remain in original jurisdiction at their new master-proceeding stages. Graham's previously identified effect on Johnson v. Texas remains a revalidation dependency. Olano has now been resolved under Rule52(b), preserving the separate narrow Chapman rule in Brecht. The May17 group is now complete, with Cardinal and its chunk7 peers each using May14 entering law. The remaining June1 chunk8 peers must use May24 entering law and exclude the completed June1 chunk7 decisions from that entering snapshot. Withrow’s remand uses law effective when applied, without treating the April 21 display order as chronology or automatically extending a Doyle holding to all Miranda issues. No future Stone choice is inferred.

### 7. Source and Research Cutoff

Opening trackers, inventory, unchanged runtime inputs and every indexed record govern continuation. All36 chunk7 split sections mechanically match the combined brief. Retrieval date September16,2026; in-world cutoffs are event-specific through June1,1993. Full official U.S.Reports opinions were reviewed, with lower-court and Archive corroboration as identified in the records. No claim is made that every retrieved filing or appendix item was independently reviewed; no unresolved source fact prevents any of the twelve reviewed dispositions. No later external follow-on event is admitted. Source limitations and statutory qualifications are preserved case by case.

Single-conversation mode: true blindness was unavailable. The neutral provisional and comparator reconciliation handoffs were frozen before the next withheld input was opened. Final records preserve the original commitments and every reconciliation, partial join and material remedial change. El Vocero and Keystone add express implementing remands to the official reversal-only decrees; those are simulated relief choices, not assertions about history. Burke’s fractured authority and Brecht’s retained Chapman rule remain the entering law. Citation-date corrections in the new drafts do not revise older records.

Validation results are in tmp/ot1992-chunk7/validation.json: input identity, judgment arithmetic, opinion membership and holding support, 120–200-word explanations, exact holding projection, eleven-block order, manifest and ledger coverage, retained open stages, chronology and protected-file hashes. No Git commit, checkout, branch or push was run.

## Current-Term Neutral Projection

**Scope and entering law:** Opening tracker versions and headers are those identified above. Current cursor:June1,1993 after the four completed chunk7 peers. The next inventory matters are FCC v.Beach Communications and Nobelman, also June1. Their entering law is the May24 state; the completed June1 decisions are not prior authority for those peers absent an independently established earlier effective sequence. May17 peers used May14 law; May24 peers used May17 law. Group members operate together prospectively after their common date.

**Court and procedure:** Nine Justices seated throughout these completed events: Chief Stone-Zsela; associates White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas in seniority order. Quorum six; ordinary full-Court action requires five of nine; certiorari requires four affirmative votes. Event-specific nonparticipation is recorded in the relevant decision and does not alter the seated roster. Allotments effective October 23, 1991 remain: Stone D.C./Fourth/Federal; Souter First; Scalia Second/Third; O'Connor Fifth; Stevens Sixth/Seventh; Blackmun Eighth; Kennedy Ninth; White Tenth; Thomas Eleventh. Listed interim applications refer to the full Court; ordinary petitions and IFP filing motions are not routed through a fictitious Circuit Justice referral.

**Current public law:** Supply the complete Current Law section above with the opening Holdings and Standards and Tests. It contains controlling propositions, named authority, complete qualifications, effective dates, precedent treatment, remedies and before-and-after effects. For any replay within a same-day group, exclude that group’s own additions. The two previously admitted statutory sources retain their recorded scope and transitions; no new noncase source is added. Do not supply records’ private Stone supplements or audit annexes to neutral modeling.

**Published noncontrolling positions:** Supply the complete Material Published Noncontrolling Positions section above. It identifies each public writing’s author, joins, proposition, scope and noncontrolling status. These positions do not acquire controlling force from inclusion in this projection.

**Current public stages:** Supply Current Procedure and Institution above and the manifest’s public statuses. Grubbs remains pending further Court order. Martin No.92-5584 is closed after certiorari denial; No.92-5618’s petition remains open on the existing paid-filing conditions, with no later payment, default or disposition inferred. Zatko, Wyoming, Reynolds and Louisiana retain their established open or implementation stages. Delaware v.New York and Nebraska v.Wyoming remain before their Special Masters. Coleman is not an OT1992 matter. Each completed chunk7 merits review is closed at this Court; specified lower-court remands are not new Supreme Court filings. Future White and Ginsburg membership transitions remain planning premises.

**Material continuation limits:** McNeil rejects cure of the original premature action but leaves the independently new-action question outside review. Sac and Fox requires actual member residence and tax predicates.
